Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quirinal_Treaty "Quirinal Treaty")[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/62/%D0%97%D1%83%D1%81%D1%82%D1%80%D1%96%D1%87_%D0%9F%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B7%D0%B8%D0%B4%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%B0_%D0%A3%D0%BA%D1%80%D0%B0%D1%97%D0%BD%D0%B8_%D0%B7_%D0%BF%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B7%D0%B8%D0%B4%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%BC%D0%B8_%D0%A4%D1%80%D0%B0%D0%BD%D1%86%D1%96%D1%97_%D1%82%D0%B0_%D0%A0%D1%83%D0%BC%D1%83%D0%BD%D1%96%D1%97%2C_%D0%B0_%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%BA%D0%BE%D0%B6_%D0%B3%D0%BE%D0%BB%D0%BE%D0%B2%D0%B0%D0%BC%D0%B8_%D1%83%D1%80%D1%8F%D0%B4%D1%96%D0%B2_%D0%9D%D1%96%D0%BC%D0%B5%D1%87%D1%87%D0%B8%D0%BD%D0%B8_%D1%82%D0%B0_%D0%86%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%BB%D1%96%D1%97_69.jpg/250px-thumbnail.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:%D0%97%D1%83%D1%81%D1%82%D1%80%D1%96%D1%87_%D0%9F%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B7%D0%B8%D0%B4%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%B0_%D0%A3%D0%BA%D1%80%D0%B0%D1%97%D0%BD%D0%B8_%D0%B7_%D0%BF%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B7%D0%B8%D0%B4%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%BC%D0%B8_%D0%A4%D1%80%D0%B0%D0%BD%D1%86%D1%96%D1%97_%D1%82%D0%B0_%D0%A0%D1%83%D0%BC%D1%83%D0%BD%D1%96%D1%97,_%D0%B0_%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%BA%D0%BE%D0%B6_%D0%B3%D0%BE%D0%BB%D0%BE%D0%B2%D0%B0%D0%BC%D0%B8_%D1%83%D1%80%D1%8F%D0%B4%D1%96%D0%B2_%D0%9D%D1%96%D0%BC%D0%B5%D1%87%D1%87%D0%B8%D0%BD%D0%B8_%D1%82%D0%B0_%D0%86%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%BB%D1%96%D1%97_69.jpg) Macron, Ukrainian President [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volodymyr_Zelenskyy "Volodymyr Zelenskyy") and German Chancellor [Olaf Scholz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olaf_Scholz "Olaf Scholz") in Kyiv in 2022

Macron attended the [2017 Brussels summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Brussels_summit "2017 Brussels summit") on 25 May 2017, his first NATO summit as president of France. At the summit, he met US President [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump") for the first time. The meeting was widely publicized due to a handshake between the two of them, characterized as a "power-struggle".[&#8202;](#ref-back-222-0)[\[220\]](#refnote-222)[&#8202;](#ref-back-223-0)[\[221\]](#refnote-223)

On 29 May 2017, Macron met with [Vladimir Putin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Putin "Vladimir Putin") at the [Palace of Versailles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_Versailles "Palace of Versailles"). The meeting sparked controversy when Macron denounced Russia Today and Sputnik, accusing the news agencies of being "organs of influence and propaganda, of lying propaganda".[&#8202;](#ref-back-224-0)[\[222\]](#refnote-224)[&#8202;](#ref-back-225-0)[\[223\]](#refnote-225) Macron also urged cooperation in the conflict against [ISIS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_State_of_Iraq_and_the_Levant "Islamic State of Iraq and the Levant") and warned that France would respond with force in Syria if chemical weapons were used.[&#8202;](#ref-back-226-0)[\[224\]](#refnote-226) In response to the [chemical attack in Douma, Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Douma_chemical_attack "Douma chemical attack") in 2018, Macron directed French participation in [airstrikes against Syrian government sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_bombing_of_Damascus_and_Homs "2018 bombing of Damascus and Homs"), coordinated with the United States and the United Kingdom.[&#8202;](#ref-back-227-0)[\[225\]](#refnote-227)[&#8202;](#ref-back-228-0)[\[226\]](#refnote-228)

In his first major foreign policy speech on 29 August, President Macron stated that fighting [Islamist terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamist_terrorism "Islamist terrorism") at home and abroad was France's top priority. Macron urged a tough international stance to pressure North Korea into negotiations, on the same day it [fired a missile over Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/August_2017_North_Korean_missile_launch_over_Japan "August 2017 North Korean missile launch over Japan"). He also affirmed his support for the [Iranian nuclear deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Comprehensive_Plan_of_Action "Joint Comprehensive Plan of Action") and criticized [Venezuela's government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_of_Venezuela "Cabinet of Venezuela") as a "dictatorship". He added that he would announce his new initiatives on the future of the European Union after the [German elections in September](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_German_federal_election "2017 German federal election").[&#8202;](#ref-back-229-0)[\[227\]](#refnote-229) At the 56th Munich Security Conference in February, Macron presented his 10-year vision policy to strengthen the European Union. Macron remarked that larger budget, integrated capital markets, effective defence policy and quick decision-making held the key for Europe. He added that reliance on NATO and especially the US and the UK was not good for Europe, and a dialogue must be established with Russia.[&#8202;](#ref-back-230-0)[\[228\]](#refnote-230)

Prior to the [45th G7 summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/45th_G7_summit "45th G7 summit") in [Biarritz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biarritz "Biarritz"), France, Macron hosted Vladimir Putin at the [Fort de Brégançon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_de_Br%C3%A9gan%C3%A7on "Fort de Brégançon"), stating that "Russia fully belongs within a Europe of values."[&#8202;](#ref-back-231-0)[\[229\]](#refnote-231) At the summit itself, Macron was invited to attend on the margins by Iranian Foreign Minister [Javad Zarif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javad_Zarif "Javad Zarif").\[ _[clarification needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify "Wikipedia:Please clarify")_\] Macron, who "attempted a high-risk diplomatic gambit", thought that the Foreign Minister of Iran might be able to defuse the tense situation over the [Iranian nuclear programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_program_of_Iran "Nuclear program of Iran") in spite of the recent uptick in tensions between the Islamic Republic and the United States and Britain.[&#8202;](#ref-back-232-0)[\[230\]](#refnote-232)

In March 2019, at a time when China–U.S. economic relations were troubled with a [trade war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China%E2%80%93United_States_trade_war "China–United States trade war") underway, Macron and Chinese leader [Xi Jinping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xi_Jinping "Xi Jinping") signed a series of 15 large-scale trade and business agreements totaling 40 billion euros (US$45 billion) which covered many sectors over a period of years.[&#8202;](#ref-back-233-0)[\[231\]](#refnote-233) This included a €30 billion purchase of airplanes from [Airbus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airbus "Airbus"). Going beyond aviation, the new trade agreement covered French exports of chicken, a French-built offshore wind farm in China, a Franco-Chinese cooperation fund, as well as billions of Euros of co-financing between [BNP Paribas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BNP_Paribas "BNP Paribas") and the [Bank of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_China "Bank of China"). Other plans included billions of euros to be spent on modernizing Chinese factories, as well as new ship building.[&#8202;](#ref-back-234-0)[\[232\]](#refnote-234) In June 2019, Macron announced that France would designate 24 April as a [national day of remembrance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Genocide_Remembrance_Day "Armenian Genocide Remembrance Day") for the [Armenian genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_genocide "Armenian genocide").[&#8202;](#ref-back-235-0)[\[233\]](#refnote-235)

In July 2020, Macron called for sanctions against [Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey "Turkey") for [violating Greece's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aegean_dispute "Aegean dispute") and [Cyprus' sovereignty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyprus_dispute "Cyprus dispute"), saying it is "unacceptable that the maritime space of (EU) member states be violated and threatened".[&#8202;](#ref-back-236-0)[\[234\]](#refnote-236) He also criticized [Turkish military intervention in Libya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_military_intervention_in_the_Second_Libyan_Civil_War "Turkish military intervention in the Second Libyan Civil War").[&#8202;](#ref-back-237-0)[\[235\]](#refnote-237)[&#8202;](#ref-back-238-0)[\[236\]](#refnote-238) Macron said that "We have the right to expect more from Turkey than from Russia, given that it is a member of NATO."[&#8202;](#ref-back-239-0)[\[237\]](#refnote-239)

In 2021, Macron was reported as saying [Northern Ireland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Ireland "Northern Ireland") was not truly part of the United Kingdom following disputes with UK Prime Minister [Boris Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boris_Johnson "Boris Johnson") over implementations of the [Northern Ireland protocol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Ireland_protocol "Northern Ireland protocol").[&#8202;](#ref-back-240-0)[\[238\]](#refnote-240) He later denied this, saying he was referring to the fact that Great Britain is separated from Northern Ireland by sea in reference to the [Irish Sea border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irish_Sea_border "Irish Sea border").[&#8202;](#ref-back-241-0)[\[239\]](#refnote-241)[&#8202;](#ref-back-242-0)[\[240\]](#refnote-242)

[French-U.S. relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France%E2%80%93United_States_relations "France–United States relations") became tense in September 2021 due to fallout from the [AUKUS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AUKUS "AUKUS") security pact between the United States, the United Kingdom, and Australia. The security pact is directed at countering [Chinese power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China "China") in the [Indo-Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-Pacific "Indo-Pacific") region. As part of the agreement, the U.S. agreed to provide [nuclear-powered submarines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear-powered_submarine "Nuclear-powered submarine") to Australia. After entering into AUKUS, the Australian government canceled an agreement that it had made with France for the [provision of French conventionally powered submarines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attack-class_submarine "Attack-class submarine"), angering the French government.[&#8202;](#ref-back-243-0)[\[241\]](#refnote-243) On 17 September, France recalled its ambassadors from Australia and the US for consultations.[&#8202;](#ref-back-recall-0)[\[242\]](#refnote-recall) Despite tension in the past, France had never before withdrawn its ambassador to the United States.[&#8202;](#ref-back-245-0)[\[243\]](#refnote-245) After a call between Macron and U.S. President [Joe Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Biden "Joe Biden") on request from the latter, the two leaders agreed to reduce bilateral tensions, and the White House acknowledged the crisis could have been averted if there had been open consultations between allies.[&#8202;](#ref-back-246-0)[\[244\]](#refnote-246)[&#8202;](#ref-back-247-0)[\[245\]](#refnote-247)\[ _[unreliable source?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Reliable_sources "Wikipedia:Reliable sources")_\]

On 26 November 2021, Macron and Italian Prime Minister [Mario Draghi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mario_Draghi "Mario Draghi") signed the [Quirinal Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quirinal_Treaty "Quirinal Treaty") at the [Quirinal Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quirinal_Palace "Quirinal Palace") in Rome.[&#8202;](#ref-back-248-0)[\[246\]](#refnote-248) The treaty aimed to promote the convergence and coordination of French and Italian positions in matters of European and foreign policies, security and defence, migration policy, economy, education, research, culture and cross-border cooperation.[&#8202;](#ref-back-249-0)[\[247\]](#refnote-249)

During the [prelude to the 2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prelude_to_the_2022_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine "Prelude to the 2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine"), Macron spoke face-to-face and on the phone to Russian President [Vladimir Putin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Putin "Vladimir Putin").[&#8202;](#ref-back-250-0)[\[248\]](#refnote-250) During Macron's campaign for the [re-election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_French_presidential_election "2022 French presidential election"), nearly two months after the [Russian invasion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine "2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine") began, Macron called on European leaders to maintain dialogue with Putin.[&#8202;](#ref-back-251-0)[\[249\]](#refnote-251)

During his 2026 address to the [UN General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_General_Assembly "UN General Assembly"), Macron claimed that [Hamas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamas "Hamas") was 'never active' in the West Bank. This caused uproar in Israel because the statements are factually inaccurate.[&#8202;](#ref-back-252-0)[\[250\]](#refnote-252)[&#8202;](#ref-back-253-0)[\[251\]](#refnote-253)

##### Approval ratings

Main article: [Opinion polling on the Emmanuel Macron presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opinion_polling_on_the_Emmanuel_Macron_presidency "Opinion polling on the Emmanuel Macron presidency")

See also: [Protests against Emmanuel Macron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protests_against_Emmanuel_Macron "Protests against Emmanuel Macron")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/Barom%C3%A8tre_politique_Macron.svg/250px-Barom%C3%A8tre_politique_Macron.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Barom%C3%A8tre_politique_Macron.svg) Approval and disapproval ratings of Macron

According to an IFOP poll for _[Le Journal du Dimanche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Journal_du_Dimanche "Le Journal du Dimanche")_, Macron started his five-year term with a 62% approval rating,[&#8202;](#ref-back-254-0)[\[252\]](#refnote-254)[&#8202;](#ref-back-255-0)[\[253\]](#refnote-255) which rose to 64% by 24 June.[&#8202;](#ref-back-256-0)[\[254\]](#refnote-256) One month later, Macron suffered a 10% point drop in popularity, the largest at the beginning of a term for any president since [Jacques Chirac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacques_Chirac "Jacques Chirac") in 1995, and by August, his popularity had fallen off 24 percentage points since June.[&#8202;](#ref-back-257-0)[\[255\]](#refnote-257) This was attributed to his recent confrontations with former Chief of Defence Staff [Pierre de Villiers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre_de_Villiers "Pierre de Villiers"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-258-0)[\[256\]](#refnote-258) the nationalization of the [Chantiers de l'Atlantique](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chantiers_de_l'Atlantique "Chantiers de l'Atlantique") shipyard owned by the bankrupt [STX Offshore & Shipbuilding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/STX_Offshore_&_Shipbuilding "STX Offshore & Shipbuilding"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-259-0)[\[257\]](#refnote-259) and a reduction in housing benefits.[&#8202;](#ref-back-260-0)[\[258\]](#refnote-260)

By the end of September 2017, seven out of ten respondents said that they believed Emmanuel Macron was respecting his campaign promises,[&#8202;](#ref-back-261-0)[\[259\]](#refnote-261)[&#8202;](#ref-back-262-0)[\[260\]](#refnote-262) though a majority felt that the policies the government was putting forward were "unfair".[&#8202;](#ref-back-263-0)[\[261\]](#refnote-263) Macron's popularity fell sharply again in 2018, reaching about 25% by the end of November during the [yellow vests movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellow_vests_movement "Yellow vests movement").[&#8202;](#ref-back-264-0)[\[262\]](#refnote-264)\[ _[unreliable source?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Reliable_sources "Wikipedia:Reliable sources")_\] During the [COVID-19 pandemic in France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_France "COVID-19 pandemic in France"), his popularity increased, reaching 50% at its highest in July 2020.[&#8202;](#ref-back-265-0)[\[263\]](#refnote-265)[&#8202;](#ref-back-266-0)[\[264\]](#refnote-266)

##### Benalla affair

Main article: [Benalla affair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benalla_affair "Benalla affair")

On 18 July 2018, _Le Monde_ revealed in an article that a member of Macron's staff [Alexandre Benalla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandre_Benalla "Alexandre Benalla") posed as a police officer and beat a protester during May Day demonstrations in Paris earlier in the year and was suspended for a period of 15 days before only being internally demoted. The Élysée failed to refer the case to the public prosecutor and a preliminary investigation into the case was not opened until the day after the publication of the article, and the lenient penalty served by Benalla raised questions within the opposition about whether the executive deliberately chose not to inform the public prosecutor as required under the code of criminal procedure.[&#8202;](#ref-back-267-0)[\[265\]](#refnote-267)

##### Social Media Regulation

In August 2026, France's Constitutional Council struck down key provisions of a proposed law prohibiting social media access for minors under 15, ruling that a blanket ban constituted a disproportionate restriction on [freedom of expression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_speech "Freedom of speech").[&#8202;](#ref-back-268-0)[\[266\]](#refnote-268) Following the decision, President Macron announced a revised legislative proposal in September 2026 to align with constitutional and European Union standards.[&#8202;](#ref-back-269-0)[\[267\]](#refnote-269)[&#8202;](#ref-back-270-0)[\[268\]](#refnote-270)[&#8202;](#ref-back-271-0)[\[269\]](#refnote-271)

#### 2022 presidential campaign

In the [2022 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_French_presidential_election "2022 French presidential election"), Macron was the first incumbent to be re-elected since Jacques Chirac defeated Jean-Marie Le Pen in the [2002 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_French_presidential_election "2002 French presidential election").[&#8202;](#ref-back-272-0)[\[270\]](#refnote-272) Macron again defeated Marine Le Pen in the runoff, this time by a closer margin, with 58.55% of the votes to Le Pen's 41.45%.[&#8202;](#ref-back-273-0)[\[271\]](#refnote-273) Due to near-record abstentions, this represented 38.52% of registered voters, the lowest figure since [Georges Pompidou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georges_Pompidou "Georges Pompidou")'s 37.5% in [1969](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1969_French_presidential_election "1969 French presidential election").[&#8202;](#ref-back-274-0)[\[272\]](#refnote-274) The French far-right received their highest vote total since the start of the French Republic, with nationalist candidates (Le Pen, [Zemmour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_Zemmour "Eric Zemmour") and [Dupont-Aignan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolas_Dupont-Aignan "Nicolas Dupont-Aignan")) winning 32.3% of the votes in the first round and Le Pen achieving a record 41.5% of the votes in the second round.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]

### Second term (2022–present)

Though Macron's second inauguration took place on 7 May 2022, his second presidential term officially began on 14 May 2022.

#### Borne government

Main article: [Borne government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borne_government "Borne government")

On 16 May 2022, Prime Minister [Jean Castex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean_Castex "Jean Castex") resigned after 22 months as head of government. The same day, President Macron appointed [Élisabeth Borne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89lisabeth_Borne "Élisabeth Borne") at the [Hôtel Matignon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%C3%B4tel_Matignon "Hôtel Matignon"), thus making her the second female PM in French history after [Édith Cresson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89dith_Cresson "Édith Cresson") between 1991 and 1992. She then formed a new government on 20 May 2022.

#### 2022 legislative election

See also: [2022 French legislative elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_French_legislative_elections "2022 French legislative elections") and [July 2022 French government reshuffle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/July_2022_French_government_reshuffle "July 2022 French government reshuffle")

In June 2022, one month into his second term, less than two weeks before the end of the French [presidency of the Council of the EU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_the_Council_of_the_European_Union "Presidency of the Council of the European Union") and days after he called for voters to hand him a "solid majority" in a controversial 'tarmac speech',[&#8202;](#ref-back-275-0)[\[273\]](#refnote-275) Macron lost his parliamentary majority and was returned a [hung parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hung_parliament "Hung parliament") in the second round of the [2022 legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_French_legislative_elections "2022 French legislative elections"):[&#8202;](#ref-back-276-0)[\[274\]](#refnote-276) Macron's [presidential coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ensemble_Citoyens "Ensemble Citoyens"), which enjoyed a 115-seat majority going into the election, failed to reach the threshold of 289 seats needed to command an overall majority in the [National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_National_Assembly "French National Assembly"), retaining only 251 out of the 346 it had held in the previous Assembly, and falling 38 short of an absolute majority.[&#8202;](#ref-back-277-0)[\[275\]](#refnote-277) Crucially, three close political allies to President Macron were defeated in the elections: incumbent President of the National Assembly [Richard Ferrand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Ferrand "Richard Ferrand"), Macron's own LREM parliamentary party leader [Christophe Castaner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christophe_Castaner "Christophe Castaner") and MoDem parliamentary group leader [Patrick Mignola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Mignola "Patrick Mignola"), thus effectively "decapitating" Macron's parliamentary bloc leadership and further weakening the President's political position in hung parliament territory.[&#8202;](#ref-back-278-0)[\[276\]](#refnote-278)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ee/National_Assembly_2022.svg/250px-National_Assembly_2022.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:National_Assembly_2022.svg) 16th National Assembly of France, elected in 2022.

Three government ministers resigned after losing their seats: [Justine Bénin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justine_B%C3%A9nin "Justine Bénin") (junior minister for the Sea), [Brigitte Bourguignon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brigitte_Bourguignon "Brigitte Bourguignon") (Minister for Health and Prevention) and [Amélie de Montchalin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Am%C3%A9lie_de_Montchalin "Amélie de Montchalin") (Minister for Ecological Transition).[&#8202;](#ref-back-279-0)[\[277\]](#refnote-279)

Macron's government, still led by Prime Minister Élisabeth Borne, was reshuffled in early July 2022 and continued as a [minority administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minority_government "Minority government"), after talks with opposition leaders to form a stable [majority government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majority_government "Majority government") failed.[&#8202;](#ref-back-280-0)[\[278\]](#refnote-280)

#### Domestic affairs

See also: [2023 French pension reform strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_French_pension_reform_strikes "2023 French pension reform strikes"), [Motions of no confidence in the government of Élisabeth Borne (2023)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motions_of_no_confidence_in_the_government_of_%C3%89lisabeth_Borne_(2023) "Motions of no confidence in the government of Élisabeth Borne (2023)"), [Nahel Merzouk riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nahel_Merzouk_riots "Nahel Merzouk riots"), [2023 French government reshuffle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_French_government_reshuffle "2023 French government reshuffle"), and [2023-2024 French government crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023-2024_French_government_crisis "2023-2024 French government crisis")

Macron's second presidential term began with two significant political controversies. Hours after the new Borne cabinet was announced, rape accusations against newly appointed Minister for Solidarity [Damien Abad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Damien_Abad "Damien Abad") were made public,[&#8202;](#ref-back-281-0)[\[279\]](#refnote-281) and on 28 May, the handling of the [2022 UEFA Champions League final chaos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_UEFA_Champions_League_final_chaos "2022 UEFA Champions League final chaos") at the Stade de France in Saint-Denis drew criticism at home and abroad.[&#8202;](#ref-back-282-0)[\[280\]](#refnote-282)

Despite its minority status in the legislature after the 2022 legislative election, Macron's government passed bills to ease the cost-of-living crisis,[&#8202;](#ref-back-283-0)[\[281\]](#refnote-283) to repeal the [COVID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_France "COVID-19 pandemic in France")-era "state of health emergency",[&#8202;](#ref-back-284-0)[\[282\]](#refnote-284) and to revive the French nuclear energy sector.[&#8202;](#ref-back-285-0)[\[283\]](#refnote-285) However, government proposals were defeated several times in the National Assembly[&#8202;](#ref-back-286-0)[\[284\]](#refnote-286) and by the end of 2022, the [Borne cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borne_government "Borne government") had had to use the provisions of [Article 49.3 of the Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Article_49_of_the_French_Constitution "Article 49 of the French Constitution") ten times in a row to pass the 2023 Government Budget and Social Security Budget.[&#8202;](#ref-back-287-0)[\[285\]](#refnote-287)

In May 2024, [protests and riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_New_Caledonia_unrest "2024 New Caledonia unrest") broke out in [New Caledonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Caledonia "New Caledonia"). On 22 May, Macron visited the island.[&#8202;](#ref-back-288-0)[\[286\]](#refnote-288)

##### Pension reform

In March 2023, Macron's government passed a law raising the retirement age from 62 to 64, partly bypassing Parliament by again resorting to Article 49.3 to break the parliamentary deadlock.[&#8202;](#ref-back-289-0)[\[287\]](#refnote-289) [Nationwide protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_French_pension_reform_unrest "2023 French pension reform unrest") that had begun when the bill was introduced back in January increased in intensity after the reform was passed without a solemn vote.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]\[ _[clarification needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify "Wikipedia:Please clarify")_\]

##### Votes of no-confidence in the Borne government

On 20 March 2023, Macron's cabinet, headed by Prime Minister Borne, survived a cross-party [motion of no-confidence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motion_of_no-confidence "Motion of no-confidence") by only nine votes, the slimmest margin for such a vote since 1992.[&#8202;](#ref-back-290-0)[\[288\]](#refnote-290)

On 12 June 2023, his government survived its 17th no-confidence motion since the beginning of the 16th legislature; the motion, brought by the left-wing NUPES coalition, fell 50 votes short of the 289 votes needed.[&#8202;](#ref-back-291-0)[\[289\]](#refnote-291)

##### Nahel Merzouk riots

In early summer 2023, French authorities faced [riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nahel_M._riots "Nahel M. riots") following the [killing of Nahel M.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Nahel_M. "Killing of Nahel M."), aged 17, by a police officer during a traffic stop.[&#8202;](#ref-back-292-0)[\[290\]](#refnote-292)[&#8202;](#ref-back-293-0)[\[291\]](#refnote-293) To calm widespread unrest, comparable in intensity to the [2005 French riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_French_riots "2005 French riots")\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\], Macron's administration ramped up government response, with a total of 45,000 police officers deployed on the ground and a ministerial order advising courts to apply harsher sentences and accelerated procedures:[&#8202;](#ref-back-294-0)[\[292\]](#refnote-294) this crackdown resulted in over 1,300 arrests on the fourth night of unrest alone, bringing the total number of arrests since the riots' beginning to over 2,000 as of 1 July.[&#8202;](#ref-back-295-0)[\[293\]](#refnote-295)

##### 2023 government reshuffle

On 20 July 2023, Macron carried out a [government reshuffle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_French_government_reshuffle "2023 French government reshuffle") at the end of the "hundred days of appeasement and action" he called for in April 2023 following the violent protests surrounding the passage of his pension system reform. [Pap Ndiaye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pap_Ndiaye "Pap Ndiaye") and [Marlène Schiappa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marl%C3%A8ne_Schiappa "Marlène Schiappa") were sacked as part of the reshuffle.[&#8202;](#ref-back-296-0)[\[294\]](#refnote-296)

##### Defence policy

On 1 August 2023, Macron signed into law a multi-year military planning bill, which set the stage for a 40%-increase in military spending to a total of €413 billion between 2024 and 2030, after it was passed by the French parliament on 13 July 2023.[&#8202;](#ref-back-297-0)[\[295\]](#refnote-297)[&#8202;](#ref-back-298-0)[\[296\]](#refnote-298)

##### Immigration policy

In February 2023, Macron's government introduced an immigration and asylum bill aimed at removing deportation safeguards, fast-tracking asylum application process and immigration litigation, while also facilitating legalization of undocumented workers.[&#8202;](#ref-back-299-0)[\[297\]](#refnote-299) His government later pulled the draft legislation amid fears of defeat in Parliament, instead planning to hold talks with the centre-right [LR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Republicans_(France) "The Republicans (France)") party before reintroducing the bill in the autumn.[&#8202;](#ref-back-300-0)[\[298\]](#refnote-300)

In August 2023, in a lengthy interview with weekly magazine _[Le Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Point "Le Point")_, Macron said that France "must significantly reduce immigration, starting with illegal immigration" because the "current situation is not sustainable".[&#8202;](#ref-back-301-0)[\[299\]](#refnote-301)

On 11 December 2023, the "flagship" immigration bill introduced by Macron's government was unexpectedly defeated after the narrow passage of a [motion for preliminary dismissal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motion_(parliamentary_procedure) "Motion (parliamentary procedure)") in the National Assembly.[&#8202;](#ref-back-302-0)[\[300\]](#refnote-302) Political commentators and news media described the vote as a "spectacular debacle", eventually sparking a major political crisis for Macron's minority administration.[&#8202;](#ref-back-303-0)[\[301\]](#refnote-303)

In an effort to salvage the bill, Macron's government sent the draft legislation to a [joint parliamentary committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_committee_(legislative) "Joint committee (legislative)"): it resulted in a deal with the conservative-controlled [Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Senate "French Senate") on a drastically hardened bill. On 19 December 2023, the French Parliament passed the piece of legislation thanks to support from the conservative [LR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Republicans_(France) "The Republicans (France)") and far-right [RN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Rally "National Rally") parliamentary groups and in spite of a major rebellion from Macron's own coalition and ministers.[&#8202;](#ref-back-304-0)[\[302\]](#refnote-304) Health Minister [Aurélien Rousseau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aur%C3%A9lien_Rousseau "Aurélien Rousseau"), whom Macron had appointed to the government only six months earlier, resigned shortly after the vote.[&#8202;](#ref-back-305-0)[\[303\]](#refnote-305)

##### Constitutional reform

On the 65th anniversary of the [French Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_the_French_Fifth_Republic "Constitution of the French Fifth Republic") on 4 October 2023, Macron unveiled avenues for constitutional reform: broadening the scope and relaxing rules for referendums; enshrining the right to abortion and climate protection in the Constitution; stepping up the level of [territorial devolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decentralisation_in_France "Decentralisation in France"); giving some form of political autonomy to [Corsica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corsica "Corsica") and [New Caledonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Caledonia "New Caledonia").[&#8202;](#ref-back-306-0)[\[304\]](#refnote-306)

On 4 March 2024, a [joint session of the French Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congress_of_the_French_Parliament "Congress of the French Parliament") passed a constitutional amendment supported by Macron to protect abortion as a 'guaranteed freedom' in the Constitution.[&#8202;](#ref-back-307-0)[\[305\]](#refnote-307) It represented the first constitutional reform since 2008 and the first since Macron took office in 2017.

##### Attal government

Main article: [Attal government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attal_government "Attal government")

In January 2024, in the wake of the [government crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023-2024_French_government_crisis "2023-2024 French government crisis") produced by the passage of the 'controversial' immigration bill, Macron requested Prime Minister Élisabeth Borne to resign and subsequently replaced her by Education minister [Gabriel Attal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabriel_Attal "Gabriel Attal"), making him both the youngest head of government in French history and the first openly gay man ever to hold the job.[&#8202;](#ref-back-308-0)[\[306\]](#refnote-308)

The new Attal cabinet was widely described as the most right-leaning government since the start of the [Macron Presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Emmanuel_Macron "Presidency of Emmanuel Macron"): out of the 14 Cabinet ministers appointed on 11 January 2024 by Macron and Attal, 57% are former members of the conservative [UMP/LR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Republicans_(France) "The Republicans (France)") party, while prominent left-leaning ministers of the outgoing Borne government were sacked, moves described as indicating a notable tilt to the right.[&#8202;](#ref-back-309-0)[\[307\]](#refnote-309)[&#8202;](#ref-back-310-0)[\[308\]](#refnote-310)

##### Economy

In February 2024, amid slowing economic growth and disappointing unemployment figures, Macron's government unveiled €10 billion in emergency spending cuts to hold to its 2024 deficit goal.[&#8202;](#ref-back-311-0)[\[309\]](#refnote-311)

A month later, Macron convened a 'crisis meeting' to discuss the state of France's public finances amid reports showing that the government had heavily missed its 2023 fiscal targets, with a bigger-than-forecasted deficit, putting the country's credit rating at risk of downgrade.[&#8202;](#ref-back-312-0)[\[310\]](#refnote-312)

#### 2024 snap legislative election

Main article: [2024 French legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_French_legislative_election "2024 French legislative election")

On 9 June 2024, following the results of the [2024 European Parliament elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_European_Parliament_elections "2024 European Parliament elections") in which his centrist grouping received only 14.6% of the votes, finishing nearly 17 points behind Le Pen's RN party, Macron unexpectedly dissolved the National Assembly and called for an [early](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snap_election "Snap election") legislative election to be held on 30 June and 7 July 2024.[&#8202;](#ref-back-313-0)[\[311\]](#refnote-313)

In the first round of the election, on 30 June, Macron's grouping received only 20.04% of the votes nationwide, placing third well behind the hard-right [RN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Rally "National Rally") (33.15%) and the left-wing [NFP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Popular_Front "New Popular Front") alliance (27.99%), which constituted the worst electoral performance for a governing coalition in a general election since the start of the modern French Republic in 1870.[&#8202;](#ref-back-314-0)[\[312\]](#refnote-314) Exit polls released shortly after 8pm on 30 June suggested the RN-led alliance was on course to win either a plurality of seats or an outright majority in the second round, while Macron's coalition was set to lose at least half of the seats it won in 2022.[&#8202;](#ref-back-315-0)[\[313\]](#refnote-315)

In the second round, on 7 July, Macron was handed another hung parliament with the left-wing coalition unexpectedly winning a plurality of seats though 90 to 100 seats short of an overall majority, leaving the left theoretically unable to form a government on its own. Macron's grouping lost both 86 seats and its status as the largest parliamentary bloc while the RN-led alliance, though dramatically underperforming the polls, still managed to make significant gains, eventually becoming the largest single party in the new National Assembly. The results presented unprecedented potential for political crisis and parliamentary gridlock.[&#8202;](#ref-back-316-0)[\[314\]](#refnote-316)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/2024_French_Parliamentary_Election_Seats.svg/250px-2024_French_Parliamentary_Election_Seats.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:2024_French_Parliamentary_Election_Seats.svg) 17th National Assembly of France, elected in 2024.

Shortly after exit polls were released on the night of 7 July, Macron's Prime Minister, Gabriel Attal, announced he would tender his resignation to the President. The following day, Macron refused Attal's resignation, instead asking him to stay on until a new government could be formed.[&#8202;](#ref-back-317-0)[\[315\]](#refnote-317)

On 27 August, Macron refused to appoint NFP-designate [Lucie Castets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucie_Castets "Lucie Castets") as prime minister.[&#8202;](#ref-back-318-0)[\[316\]](#refnote-318)

After holding talks with leaders from parties represented in Parliament in an effort to find a working majority, Macron appointed conservative figure [Michel Barnier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michel_Barnier "Michel Barnier") as Prime Minister on 5 September and invited him to form a "unity government". Barnier's appointment was seen as a growing sign of Le Pen's influence on French politics since she had previously blocked the appointment of [Xavier Bertrand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xavier_Bertrand "Xavier Bertrand"), another conservative figure seen as a serious contender for the premiership.[&#8202;](#ref-back-319-0)[\[317\]](#refnote-319)

##### Vote of no-confidence in the Barnier government

In early December 2024, Prime Minister Michel Barnier resorted to Article 49.3 of the Constitution to pass the 2025 Social Security budget without parliamentary approval. In response to Barnier's move, the National Assembly approved a vote of no confidence on 4 December, effectively bringing down the government. Barnier resigned, but Macron vowed to stay in office until the end of his term.[&#8202;](#ref-back-320-0)[\[318\]](#refnote-320) It was the first French cabinet to be toppled by Parliament since 1962.[&#8202;](#ref-back-321-0)[\[319\]](#refnote-321) Macron addressed the country on 5 December. He accepted the resignation of Barnier but vowed to remain in office until the end of his term. He blamed an alliance of 'extreme left and extreme right' for the collapse of the French government.[&#8202;](#ref-back-322-0)[\[320\]](#refnote-322)

#### Foreign affairs

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/Meloni_and_Macron_2022.jpg/250px-Meloni_and_Macron_2022.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Meloni_and_Macron_2022.jpg) Macron with Italian Prime Minister [Giorgia Meloni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giorgia_Meloni "Giorgia Meloni") in October 2022[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/41/Emmanuel_Macron%2C_Xi_Jinping_and_Ursula_von_der_Leyen_in_Beijing_-_2023.jpg/250px-Emmanuel_Macron%2C_Xi_Jinping_and_Ursula_von_der_Leyen_in_Beijing_-_2023.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Emmanuel_Macron,_Xi_Jinping_and_Ursula_von_der_Leyen_in_Beijing_-_2023.jpg) Macron, President of the European Commission [Ursula von der Leyen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ursula_von_der_Leyen "Ursula von der Leyen") and Chinese President [Xi Jinping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xi_Jinping "Xi Jinping") at the [2023 France–China Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_France%E2%80%93China_Summit "2023 France–China Summit")

On 16 June 2022, Macron visited Ukraine alongside German Chancellor [Olaf Scholz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olaf_Scholz "Olaf Scholz") and Italy's Prime Minister [Mario Draghi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mario_Draghi "Mario Draghi"). He met with Ukraine's President [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volodymyr_Zelenskyy "Volodymyr Zelenskyy") and expressed "European Unity" for Ukraine.[&#8202;](#ref-back-323-0)[\[321\]](#refnote-323)[&#8202;](#ref-back-324-0)[\[322\]](#refnote-324) He said that the nations that remained [neutral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement "Non-Aligned Movement") in the [Russo-Ukrainian War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russo-Ukrainian_War "Russo-Ukrainian War") made a historic mistake and were complicit in a new imperialism.[&#8202;](#ref-back-325-0)[\[323\]](#refnote-325)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5e/20.05.2023_-_Encontro_com_o_Presidente_da_Rep%C3%BAblica_Francesa%2C_Emmanuel_Macron_%2852912156553%29.jpg/250px-20.05.2023_-_Encontro_com_o_Presidente_da_Rep%C3%BAblica_Francesa%2C_Emmanuel_Macron_%2852912156553%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:20.05.2023_-_Encontro_com_o_Presidente_da_Rep%C3%BAblica_Francesa,_Emmanuel_Macron_(52912156553).jpg) Macron with Brazilian President [Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva "Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva") in May 2023

In September 2022, Macron criticized the United States, [Norway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norway "Norway") and other "friendly" [natural gas supplier states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_natural_gas_exports "List of countries by natural gas exports") for the extremely high prices of their supplies,[&#8202;](#ref-back-326-0)[\[324\]](#refnote-326) saying in October 2022 that Europeans are "paying four times as much as the price at which you sell to your industry. That is not exactly the meaning of friendship."[&#8202;](#ref-back-327-0)[\[325\]](#refnote-327)

Macron and his wife attended the [state funeral of Queen Elizabeth II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_and_state_funeral_of_Elizabeth_II "Death and state funeral of Elizabeth II") in Westminster Abbey, London, on 19 September 2022, and the [coronation of King Charles III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronation_of_Charles_III_and_Camilla "Coronation of Charles III and Camilla") the following year.[&#8202;](#ref-back-328-0)[\[326\]](#refnote-328)[&#8202;](#ref-back-329-0)[\[327\]](#refnote-329)

On 23 October 2022, Macron became the first foreign leader to meet new Italian Prime Minister [Giorgia Meloni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giorgia_Meloni "Giorgia Meloni"), just a day after she and her [ministers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meloni_government "Meloni government") were sworn into office.[&#8202;](#ref-back-330-0)[\[328\]](#refnote-330)

During a [summit to China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_France-China_Summit "2023 France-China Summit") with European Commission President [Ursula von der Leyen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ursula_von_der_Leyen "Ursula von der Leyen"), which included a formal meeting with [Xi Jinping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xi_Jinping "Xi Jinping"), the [General Secretary of the Chinese Communist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Secretary_of_the_Chinese_Communist_Party "General Secretary of the Chinese Communist Party") and [President of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_China "President of China"), Macron called for Europe to reduce its dependence on the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States "United States") in general and to stay neutral and avoid being drawn into any possible confrontation between the U.S. and [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China "China") over [Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan "Taiwan"). Speaking after a three-day state visit to China, Macron emphasised his theory of [strategic autonomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strategic_autonomy "Strategic autonomy"), suggesting that Europe could become a " [third superpower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potential_superpower#European_Union "Potential superpower")". He argued that Europe should focus on boosting its own defence industries and additionally reduce its dependence on the [United States dollar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_dollar "United States dollar") (USD).[&#8202;](#ref-back-peuja-0)[\[329\]](#refnote-peuja) in a follow-up speech in [The Hague](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hague "The Hague") to further outline his vision of strategic autonomy for Europe.[&#8202;](#ref-back-f24out-0)[\[330\]](#refnote-f24out) On 7 June 2023, a report by the [pan-European](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan-European_identity "Pan-European identity") [think tank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Think_tank "Think tank") [European Council on Foreign Relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Council_on_Foreign_Relations "European Council on Foreign Relations") (ECFR) found that most Europeans agree with Macron's views on China and the United States.[&#8202;](#ref-back-333-0)[\[331\]](#refnote-333)

In February 2023, he welcomed Ethiopian Prime Minister [Abiy Ahmed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abiy_Ahmed "Abiy Ahmed") in Paris to normalize relations between France and [Ethiopia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethiopia "Ethiopia"), strained by the [Tigray War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tigray_War "Tigray War") between the Ethiopian government and [Tigray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tigray_Region "Tigray Region") rebels.[&#8202;](#ref-back-334-0)[\[332\]](#refnote-334)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a2/Prime_minister_Rishi_Sunak_attends_the_NATO_Summit_in_Lithuania_%2853041237495%29.jpg/250px-Prime_minister_Rishi_Sunak_attends_the_NATO_Summit_in_Lithuania_%2853041237495%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Prime_minister_Rishi_Sunak_attends_the_NATO_Summit_in_Lithuania_(53041237495).jpg) Macron at the [NATO Summit in Vilnius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Vilnius_NATO_summit "2023 Vilnius NATO summit") on 12 July 2023

On 31 May 2023 Macron visited the GLOBSEC forum in Bratislava, where he again delivered a speech on European sovereignty.[&#8202;](#ref-back-f24slv-0)[\[333\]](#refnote-f24slv) During the question and answer session that followed the Bratislava speech,[&#8202;](#ref-back-336-0)[\[334\]](#refnote-336) he said that negotiating with Putin may have to take priority over any war crimes tribunal which some others, including Zelenskyy, wish to see.[&#8202;](#ref-back-pwput-0)[\[335\]](#refnote-pwput)

On 12 June 2023, Macron promised to deliver more ammunition, weapons and armed vehicles to help Ukrainian forces with the ongoing [counter-offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Ukrainian_counteroffensive "2023 Ukrainian counteroffensive") to liberate Russian- [occupied](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_annexation_of_Donetsk,_Kherson,_Luhansk_and_Zaporizhzhia_oblasts "Russian annexation of Donetsk, Kherson, Luhansk and Zaporizhzhia oblasts") southeastern [Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine "Ukraine").[&#8202;](#ref-back-338-0)[\[336\]](#refnote-338) At the [NATO Summit in Vilnius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Vilnius_summit "2023 Vilnius summit"), he promised to supply Ukraine with [Scalp long-range cruise missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Storm_Shadow "Storm Shadow") to hit Russian targets deep behind the front lines.[&#8202;](#ref-back-339-0)[\[337\]](#refnote-339) On 10 November 2023, he said that what Russia is doing in Ukraine is "imperialism and colonialism" and it was the "duty" of France and other countries to help Ukraine defend itself, but added that maybe the time will come to hold fair [peace negotiations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_negotiations_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine "Peace negotiations in the Russian invasion of Ukraine") and find a solution with Russia.[&#8202;](#ref-back-340-0)[\[338\]](#refnote-340)

In June 2023 Macron hosted a global climate finance conference described by many as the new [Bretton Woods Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bretton_Woods_Conference "Bretton Woods Conference"). The purpose is to adjust the global economy to the contemporary threats of [climate change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change "Climate change") and hunger. One of the propositions is to offer low income countries aid instead of credit so they can use their resources for stopping climate change and poverty instead of debt payments. Macron supported the idea, but a climate activist from Uganda remarked that the promises were meaningless if at the same time Macron supported projects such as the [East African Crude Oil Pipeline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_African_Crude_Oil_Pipeline "East African Crude Oil Pipeline"), a major threat to the climate and to the drinking water of 40 million people.[&#8202;](#ref-back-341-0)[\[339\]](#refnote-341) At the summit Macron proposed an [international taxation system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_taxation "International taxation") and [debt restructuring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Debt_restructuring "Debt restructuring") but stressed it can have an effect only with [international cooperation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_cooperation "International cooperation").[&#8202;](#ref-back-342-0)[\[340\]](#refnote-342)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/Isaac_Herzog_in_Beit_HaNassi%2C_October_2023_%28ABG_7050%29.jpg/250px-Isaac_Herzog_in_Beit_HaNassi%2C_October_2023_%28ABG_7050%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Isaac_Herzog_in_Beit_HaNassi,_October_2023_(ABG_7050).jpg) Macron with Israeli President [Isaac Herzog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isaac_Herzog "Isaac Herzog") in Jerusalem, Israel, 24 October 2023

In July 2023, Macron postponed his planned state visit to Germany due to the ongoing [Nahel M. riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nahel_M._riots "Nahel M. riots").[&#8202;](#ref-back-343-0)[\[341\]](#refnote-343)

In October 2023, Macron condemned [Hamas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamas "Hamas")'s actions during the [Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war "Gaza war") and expressed his support to [Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel "Israel") and its right to self-defense.[&#8202;](#ref-back-344-0)[\[342\]](#refnote-344)[&#8202;](#ref-back-345-0)[\[343\]](#refnote-345) On 10 November 2023, he called for a ceasefire and urged Israel to stop [bombing Gaza and killing civilians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_crimes_in_the_2023_Israel%E2%80%93Hamas_war "War crimes in the 2023 Israel–Hamas war").[&#8202;](#ref-back-346-0)[\[344\]](#refnote-346) In July 2025, Macron announced that at the next meeting of the [UN General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_General_Assembly "UN General Assembly"), France would officially recognise a Palestinian state.[&#8202;](#ref-back-347-0)[\[345\]](#refnote-347)

In February 2024, during a meeting with other European states, Macron generated controversy by suggesting [sending ground troops to Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine%E2%80%93NATO_relations "Ukraine–NATO relations").[&#8202;](#ref-back-348-0)[\[346\]](#refnote-348) On 28 May 2024, Macron gave Ukraine permission to use [SCALP EG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SCALP_EG "SCALP EG") missiles against targets on Russian soil. Such usage was instructed to be limited "to neutralize military sites from which missiles are being fired, military sites from which Ukraine is being attacked".[&#8202;](#ref-back-349-0)[\[347\]](#refnote-349) On 24 February 2025, during a meeting with US President [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump"), Macron said that a [truce between Ukraine and Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_negotiations_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine "Peace negotiations in the Russian invasion of Ukraine") could be agreed in the coming weeks.[&#8202;](#ref-back-350-0)[\[348\]](#refnote-350) On 22 February 2025, Macron pledged to continue its supply of defense equipment to [Armenia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenia "Armenia") after the 2020 Nagorno-Karabakh war, as well as humanitarian aid of 29 million euros to [displaced Armenians from Nagorno-Karabakh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expulsion_of_Nagorno-Karabakh_Armenians "Expulsion of Nagorno-Karabakh Armenians"). He also stated support for the [International Court of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Court_of_Justice "International Court of Justice")'s decision on 17 November 2023, of which calls on the rights of ethnic Armenian residents to return safely to their homeland.[&#8202;](#ref-back-351-0)[\[349\]](#refnote-351)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/92/Prime_Minister_Keir_Starmer_visits_Ukraine_%2854508511672%29.jpg/250px-Prime_Minister_Keir_Starmer_visits_Ukraine_%2854508511672%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Prime_Minister_Keir_Starmer_visits_Ukraine_(54508511672).jpg) German Chancellor [Friedrich Merz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friedrich_Merz "Friedrich Merz"), Macron, British Prime Minister [Keir Starmer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keir_Starmer "Keir Starmer") and Polish Prime Minister [Donald Tusk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Tusk "Donald Tusk") with Ukrainian President [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volodymyr_Zelenskyy "Volodymyr Zelenskyy") in [Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine "Ukraine") in May 2025

In February 2025, Macron characterised Russia as an "existential threat" to Europe, convening a special meeting in Paris to coordinate European responses under the [Weimar+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weimar+ "Weimar+") framework.[&#8202;](#ref-back-352-0)[\[350\]](#refnote-352) In March 2025, German lawmakers approved an [amendment to the Basic Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_balanced_budget_amendment "German balanced budget amendment") that would allow [Friedrich Merz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friedrich_Merz "Friedrich Merz")'s government to implement the most massive rearmament of Germany since [World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_II "World War II").[&#8202;](#ref-back-353-0)[\[351\]](#refnote-353) Germany's decision to massively [increase defense spending](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020s_European_rearmament "2020s European rearmament") was welcomed by Macron.[&#8202;](#ref-back-354-0)[\[352\]](#refnote-354)

Following the [Israeli strikes on Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/June_2025_Israeli_strikes_on_Iran "June 2025 Israeli strikes on Iran") on 13 June 2025, Macron called for "maximum restraint" while reaffirming "Israel's right to defend itself and ensure its security."[&#8202;](#ref-back-355-0)[\[353\]](#refnote-355) On 17 June 2025, he claimed that president Trump's haste departure from the [G7 summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G7 "G7"), was to reach a cease fire between Israel and Iran. To which Trump responded "Wrong! He has no idea why I am now on my way to Washington, but it certainly has nothing to do with a Cease Fire. Much bigger than that. Whether purposely or not, Emmanuel always gets it wrong. Stay Tuned!".[&#8202;](#ref-back-356-0)[\[354\]](#refnote-356) On 23 June 2025, Macron called Trump's [air strikes on Iranian nuclear sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_United_States_strikes_on_Iranian_nuclear_sites "2025 United States strikes on Iranian nuclear sites") "illegal" and counterproductive,[&#8202;](#ref-back-357-0)[\[355\]](#refnote-357) and called for the resumption of diplomatic talks with Iran.[&#8202;](#ref-back-358-0)[\[356\]](#refnote-358)

In 2026, speaking at the Africa Forward Summit in Nairobi, Kenya, Macron called for an immediate ceasefire in the [Sudanese civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sudanese_civil_war_(2023%E2%80%93present) "Sudanese civil war (2023–present)") and an end to external support for the belligerents. He stated that the conflict was being fuelled by external forces financing the warring parties and described the situation as involving [war crimes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_crimes_during_the_Sudanese_civil_war_(2023%E2%80%93present) "War crimes during the Sudanese civil war (2023–present)") and a severe [humanitarian crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humanitarian_impact_of_the_Sudanese_civil_war_(2023%E2%80%93present) "Humanitarian impact of the Sudanese civil war (2023–present)").[&#8202;](#ref-back-359-0)[\[357\]](#refnote-359) During his presidency, France faced criticism after [Amnesty International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amnesty_International "Amnesty International") reported that French-manufactured military systems were being used by Sudan’s [Rapid Support Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapid_Support_Forces "Rapid Support Forces") (RSF) on armoured vehicles supplied by the [United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Arab_Emirates "United Arab Emirates").[&#8202;](#ref-back-360-0)[\[358\]](#refnote-360) Amnesty called on France to suspend further transfers of the equipment to the United Arab Emirates.[&#8202;](#ref-back-361-0)[\[359\]](#refnote-361)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/President_Donald_Trump_meets_with_French_President_Emmanuel_Macron_at_the_United_Nations_Headquarters_%2854823840529%29.jpg/250px-President_Donald_Trump_meets_with_French_President_Emmanuel_Macron_at_the_United_Nations_Headquarters_%2854823840529%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:President_Donald_Trump_meets_with_French_President_Emmanuel_Macron_at_the_United_Nations_Headquarters_(54823840529).jpg) Macron and U.S. President [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump") at the UN General Assembly in New York City, 23 September 2025

In January 2026, following the [capture of Nicolás Maduro by U.S. forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_United_States_strikes_in_Venezuela "2026 United States strikes in Venezuela"), Macron formally called for [Edmundo González](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmundo_Gonz%C3%A1lez "Edmundo González") to lead a "peaceful, democratic" transition in [Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuela "Venezuela").[&#8202;](#ref-back-362-0)[\[360\]](#refnote-362) Macron praised the removal of the "Maduro dictatorship," stating that the Venezuelan people "can only rejoice" at being rid of his rule.[&#8202;](#ref-back-363-0)[\[361\]](#refnote-363)

On 9 January 2026, Macron, along with British Prime Minister [Keir Starmer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keir_Starmer "Keir Starmer") and German Chancellor [Friedrich Merz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friedrich_Merz "Friedrich Merz"), issued a joint statement addressing the escalating [anti-government protests in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025%E2%80%932026_Iranian_protests "2025–2026 Iranian protests"), which had started in late December 2025. In their statement, they called on the Iranian authorities to exercise restraint and to avoid further [violence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Iran_massacres "2026 Iran massacres").[&#8202;](#ref-back-364-0)[\[362\]](#refnote-364)

During his speech at the [World Economic Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/56th_World_Economic_Forum "56th World Economic Forum") in Davos on 20 January 2026, Macron emphasized the importance of increasing Chinese [foreign direct investment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_direct_investment "Foreign direct investment") (FDI) in key European sectors to promote economic growth and facilitate technology transfer. He urged [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China "China") to move beyond simply exporting products to Europe and instead focus on local manufacturing and physical presence on the continent.[&#8202;](#ref-back-365-0)[\[363\]](#refnote-365)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ef/Emmanuel_Macron%2C_Ahmed_al-Sharaa%2C_and_Asaad_al-Shaibani%2C_April_2026.jpg/250px-Emmanuel_Macron%2C_Ahmed_al-Sharaa%2C_and_Asaad_al-Shaibani%2C_April_2026.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Emmanuel_Macron,_Ahmed_al-Sharaa,_and_Asaad_al-Shaibani,_April_2026.jpg) Macron, Syrian President [Ahmed al-Sharaa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmed_al-Sharaa "Ahmed al-Sharaa"), and Syrian Foreign Minister [Asaad al-Shaibani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asaad_al-Shaibani "Asaad al-Shaibani"), 24 April 2026

In March 2026, Macron described the [U.S.–Israeli strikes on Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Iran_war "2026 Iran war") as illegal, while maintaining that Iran held "primary responsibility" for the regional escalation. Despite criticizing the strikes, he reaffirmed France's commitment to regional allies and deployed the aircraft carrier _[Charles de Gaulle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_aircraft_carrier_Charles_de_Gaulle "French aircraft carrier Charles de Gaulle")_ to the Mediterranean for defensive and escort missions.[&#8202;](#ref-back-366-0)[\[364\]](#refnote-366) In September 2026, Macron announced that the country will send soldiers to [protect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France%E2%80%93Saudi_Arabia_relations "France–Saudi Arabia relations") Saudi Arabia on a [Red Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Sea "Red Sea") oil route, as part of an accord with Saudis.[&#8202;](#ref-back-367-0)[\[365\]](#refnote-367)

#### Controversies

##### Uber Files

Main article: [Uber Files](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uber_Files "Uber Files")

On 10 July 2022, _[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian "The Guardian")_ revealed that Macron had assisted Uber in lobbying during his term as the Minister of Economics and Finance,[&#8202;](#ref-back-GuardianUberFiles-0)[\[366\]](#refnote-GuardianUberFiles) leading to calls from opposition lawmakers for a parliamentary inquiry.[&#8202;](#ref-back-UberFilesInquiryCalls-0)[\[367\]](#refnote-UberFilesInquiryCalls)[&#8202;](#ref-back-UberDefence-0)[\[368\]](#refnote-UberDefence) In his own defence, Macron expressed that he "did his job" and that he would "do it again tomorrow and the day after tomorrow".[&#8202;](#ref-back-UberDefence-1)[\[368\]](#refnote-UberDefence) He stated, "I'm proud of it".[&#8202;](#ref-back-UberDefence-2)[\[368\]](#refnote-UberDefence)

### Co-prince of Andorra

As president of France, Macron also serves _ex officio_ as one of the two [co-princes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Co-Prince_of_Andorra "Co-Prince of Andorra") of [Andorra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andorra "Andorra"). His chief of staff [Patrick Strzoda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Strzoda "Patrick Strzoda") serves as his representative in this capacity. [Joan Enric Vives i Sicília](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joan_Enric_Vives_i_Sic%C3%ADlia "Joan Enric Vives i Sicília"), appointed as the [Bishop of Urgell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bishop_of_Urgell "Bishop of Urgell") on 12 May 2003, served as Macron's co-prince until [Josep-Lluís Serrano Pentinat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josep-Llu%C3%ADs_Serrano_Pentinat "Josep-Lluís Serrano Pentinat") succeeded him in 2025.[&#8202;](#ref-back-371-0)[\[369\]](#refnote-371) Macron swore the [Constitution of Andorra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Andorra "Constitution of Andorra") through Strzoda in an act that took place on 15 June 2017 in [Casa de la Vall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casa_de_la_Vall "Casa de la Vall").[&#8202;](#ref-back-372-0)[\[370\]](#refnote-372) Macron visited the country on a State visit on 12 September 2019. The following day he spoke at the historic building of the [General Council of Andorra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Council_(Andorra) "General Council (Andorra)") alongside Episcopal Co-Prince Joan Enric Vives, Prime Minister [Xavier Espot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xavier_Espot "Xavier Espot") and the General Syndic [Roser Suñé Pascuet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roser_Su%C3%B1%C3%A9_Pascuet "Roser Suñé Pascuet").[&#8202;](#ref-back-373-0)[\[371\]](#refnote-373)

During the [COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic "COVID-19 pandemic"), the Andorran government asked France for [economic aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aid "Aid"), but Macron refused, arguing that the [Bank of France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_France "Bank of France") could not offer loans to another country without the approval of the [European Central Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Central_Bank "European Central Bank").[&#8202;](#ref-back-374-0)[\[372\]](#refnote-374)

In January 2024, former New Caledonian high commissioner [Patrice Faure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrice_Faure "Patrice Faure") was appointed chief of staff to Macron,[&#8202;](#ref-back-375-0)[\[373\]](#refnote-375) and in November, his representative to [Andorra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andorra "Andorra").[&#8202;](#ref-back-376-0)[\[374\]](#refnote-376)

## Political positions

Main article: [Political positions of Emmanuel Macron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_positions_of_Emmanuel_Macron "Political positions of Emmanuel Macron")

Macron's political views have been described as [moderate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moderate "Moderate"), [centrist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centrist "Centrist"), and [pragmatic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pragmatic_politics "Pragmatic politics").

## Personal life

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Emmanuel_et_Brigitte_Macron_%28cropped%29.jpg/250px-Emmanuel_et_Brigitte_Macron_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Emmanuel_et_Brigitte_Macron_(cropped).jpg) Emmanuel Macron and his wife [Brigitte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brigitte_Macron "Brigitte Macron") in 2017

Macron is married to [Brigitte Trogneux](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brigitte_Macron "Brigitte Macron").[&#8202;](#ref-back-377-0)[\[375\]](#refnote-377) She is his former [La Providence High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyc%C3%A9e_la_Providence "Lycée la Providence") teacher and is close to 25 years his senior.[&#8202;](#ref-back-378-0)[\[376\]](#refnote-378)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Atlantic-0)[\[377\]](#refnote-Atlantic)[&#8202;](#ref-back-380-0)[\[378\]](#refnote-380) They met during a theatre workshop that she was giving when she was a 39-year-old teacher and he was a 15-year-old student and classmate of her daughter.[&#8202;](#ref-back-381-0)[\[379\]](#refnote-381)[&#8202;](#ref-back-382-0)[\[380\]](#refnote-382)[&#8202;](#ref-back-internaute-0)[\[381\]](#refnote-internaute) A romantic relationship started later, and his parents attempted to separate the couple by sending him away to Paris to finish the final year of his schooling, as they felt his youth made this relationship inappropriate.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Chrisafis-1)[\[16\]](#refnote-Chrisafis)[&#8202;](#ref-back-internaute-1)[\[381\]](#refnote-internaute) (The [age of consent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_of_consent_in_Europe "Age of consent in Europe") in France is 15.) The couple reunited after Macron graduated and were married in 2007.[&#8202;](#ref-back-internaute-2)[\[381\]](#refnote-internaute) She has three children from a previous marriage;[&#8202;](#ref-back-384-0)[\[382\]](#refnote-384) he has no children of his own.[&#8202;](#ref-back-385-0)[\[383\]](#refnote-385) Her role in Macron's 2017 presidential campaign has been considered pivotal, with close Macron allies stating that Brigitte Macron helped him to develop skills such as [public speaking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_speaking "Public speaking").[&#8202;](#ref-back-386-0)[\[384\]](#refnote-386)

His [best man](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best_man "Best man") was [Henry Hermand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Hermand "Henry Hermand"), a businessman who loaned €550,000 to Macron for the purchase of his first apartment in [Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris "Paris") when he was Inspector of Finances. Hermand also let Macron use some of his offices on the [Avenue des Champs Élysées](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Champs_%C3%89lys%C3%A9es "Champs Élysées") in Paris for his movement [En Marche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/En_Marche "En Marche").[&#8202;](#ref-back-lemondeobit-0)[\[385\]](#refnote-lemondeobit)[&#8202;](#ref-back-libeobit-0)[\[386\]](#refnote-libeobit)

In the [2002 French presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_French_presidential_election "2002 French presidential election"), Macron voted for [souverainist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Souverainism "Souverainism") [Jean-Pierre Chevènement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Pierre_Chev%C3%A8nement "Jean-Pierre Chevènement").[&#8202;](#ref-back-389-0)[\[387\]](#refnote-389) In 2007 Macron voted for [Ségolène Royal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A9gol%C3%A8ne_Royal "Ségolène Royal") in the second round of the [presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_French_presidential_election "2007 French presidential election").[&#8202;](#ref-back-390-0)[\[388\]](#refnote-390) During the [Socialist Party primary in 2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Socialist_Party_presidential_primary,_2011 "French Socialist Party presidential primary, 2011"), Macron voiced his support for [François Hollande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois_Hollande "François Hollande").[&#8202;](#ref-back-391-0)[\[389\]](#refnote-391)

Macron plays the [piano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piano "Piano"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-392-0)[\[390\]](#refnote-392) which he studied for ten years in his youth.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Neva_Editions_2015,_p.193-1)[\[17\]](#refnote-Neva_Editions_2015,_p.193) He especially enjoys the work of [Robert Schumann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Schumann "Robert Schumann") and [Franz Liszt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franz_Liszt "Franz Liszt").[&#8202;](#ref-back-393-0)[\[391\]](#refnote-393)[&#8202;](#ref-back-394-0)[\[392\]](#refnote-394) Macron also skis,[&#8202;](#ref-back-395-0)[\[393\]](#refnote-395) plays [tennis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis "Tennis"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-396-0)[\[394\]](#refnote-396) and enjoys [boxing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boxing "Boxing").[&#8202;](#ref-back-397-0)[\[395\]](#refnote-397) In addition to his native French, Macron speaks fluent English.[&#8202;](#ref-back-398-0)[\[396\]](#refnote-398)[&#8202;](#ref-back-399-0)[\[397\]](#refnote-399)

In August 2017, a photojournalist was arrested and detained by the police for six hours after he entered the private residence where Macron was vacationing in [Marseille](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marseille "Marseille").[&#8202;](#ref-back-valeursactuellesporteplainte-0)[\[398\]](#refnote-valeursactuellesporteplainte) Macron subsequently filed a complaint for " [harassment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harassment "Harassment")".[&#8202;](#ref-back-valeursactuellesporteplainte-1)[\[398\]](#refnote-valeursactuellesporteplainte) In September 2017, he dropped the complaint "as a gesture of appeasement".[&#8202;](#ref-back-lexpansionmacronretiresaplainte-0)[\[399\]](#refnote-lexpansionmacronretiresaplainte)

On 27 August 2017, Macron and his wife Brigitte adopted [Nemo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nemo_(dog) "Nemo (dog)"), a black Labrador Retriever-Griffon dog who lives with them in the Élysée Palace.[&#8202;](#ref-back-lefigunnouveauchienalelysee-0)[\[400\]](#refnote-lefigunnouveauchienalelysee)

When he was a schoolboy, Macron decided to be baptised as a Catholic. In June 2018, prior to meeting [Pope Francis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Francis "Pope Francis"), he identified himself as an [agnostic Catholic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lapsed_Catholic "Lapsed Catholic").[&#8202;](#ref-back-403-0)[\[401\]](#refnote-403)[&#8202;](#ref-back-spotlight-0)[\[402\]](#refnote-spotlight) In the same year he agreed to become an honorary canon of [St John Lateran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_John_Lateran "St John Lateran"), the cathedral of Rome.[&#8202;](#ref-back-spotlight-1)[\[402\]](#refnote-spotlight)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0a/2018_World_Cup_Final_%282018-07-15%29_02.jpg/330px-2018_World_Cup_Final_%282018-07-15%29_02.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:2018_World_Cup_Final_(2018-07-15)_02.jpg) Macron celebrating France's victory over [Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatia_national_football_team "Croatia national football team") in the [2018 World Cup final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_FIFA_World_Cup_Final "2018 FIFA World Cup Final") in Moscow, Russia

A fan of [football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_football "Association football"), Macron is a supporter of French club [Olympique de Marseille](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympique_de_Marseille "Olympique de Marseille").[&#8202;](#ref-back-405-0)[\[403\]](#refnote-405) During the [2018 World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_FIFA_World_Cup "2018 FIFA World Cup"), he attended the semi-final between [France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France_national_football_team "France national football team") and [Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgium_national_football_team "Belgium national football team") with the Belgian [King Philippe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippe_of_Belgium "Philippe of Belgium") and [Queen Mathilde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_Mathilde_of_Belgium "Queen Mathilde of Belgium");[&#8202;](#ref-back-406-0)[\[404\]](#refnote-406) and at the [World Cup final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_FIFA_World_Cup_Final "2018 FIFA World Cup Final") against [Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatia_national_football_team "Croatia national football team"), he sat and celebrated alongside Croatian president [Kolinda Grabar-Kitarović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolinda_Grabar-Kitarovi%C4%87 "Kolinda Grabar-Kitarović"). Macron received widespread media attention for his celebrations and his interactions with the Croatian president.[&#8202;](#ref-back-407-0)[\[405\]](#refnote-407)

At a new year's celebration for military forces on 15 January 2026, Macron was seen with a bulging eye. The condition is known as a " [sub-conjunctival haemorrhage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sub-conjunctival_haemorrhage "Sub-conjunctival haemorrhage")". He chose to wear aviator sunglasses in public spaces to obscure this injury.[&#8202;](#ref-back-408-0)[\[406\]](#refnote-408) After gaining attention for wearing aviator sunglasses while speaking at the [World Economic Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Economic_Forum "World Economic Forum") in [Davos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davos "Davos") on 20 January, demand for Henry Jullien sunglasses spiked. The brand's website crashed and its parent company, iVision, saw its [stock price](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stock_price "Stock price") increase by 28%.[&#8202;](#ref-back-409-0)[\[407\]](#refnote-409)[&#8202;](#ref-back-410-0)[\[408\]](#refnote-410)
